sidebar:

---

import { LinkCard } from "@astrojs/starlight/components";

:::note
The first section of the Padarūpasiddhi gathers Kaccāyana's definitions (`saññā`) and the sandhi rules of the Sandhikappa in Buddhappiya's own order — vowel sandhi, the cases where the vowel stays unchanged (`pakati`), consonant sandhi, niggahīta sandhi — and derives every example word it cites, quoting the rules by their wording. Each sutta carries the Kaccāyana number it corresponds to and a card to the Kaccāyana page, where the rule's own analysis, examples and counter-examples are.

:::

:::note[The five parts of the Sandhikaṇḍa]
Buddhappiya's order for the sandhi rules, with the Rūpasiddhi numbers and the Kaccāyana rules each part draws on.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  S(["Sandhikaṇḍa 1–59"]) --> A["Saññāvidhāna 1–11<br/>letters, vowels, consonants, the terms<br/>§1–§9, with §602–§603 on heavy syllables"]
  S --> B["Sarasandhividhāna 12–34<br/>a vowel before a vowel: elision 13, the option 15, lengthening 17–18,<br/>y and v for e and o 19–20, insertion 34<br/>§10–§22, §35, §42–§47 and three noun rules"]
  S --> C["Pakatisandhividhāna 35–36<br/>the vowel unchanged §23–§24"]
  S --> D["Byañjanasandhividhāna 37–48<br/>a vowel before a consonant: long, short or elided 37–39,<br/>doubling 40 and 42, o for ava 45<br/>§25–§29, §36, §48–§50 and three noun rules"]
  S --> E["Niggahītasandhividhāna 49–59<br/>ṃ before vowels and consonants, and the closing rule 59<br/>§30–§34, §37–§41, §51"]
```

:::

> Tattha jinasāsanādhigamassa akkharakosallamūlakattā taṃ sampādetabbanti dassetuṃ abhidheyyappayojanavākyamidamuccate.

There [after the opening verses], since the understanding of the Conqueror's teaching has skill in letters (`akkharakosalla`) as its root, this sentence, which states the subject-matter (`abhidheyya`) and the purpose (`payojana`) [of the grammar], is spoken in order to show that that [skill] is to be acquired.

---

<a name="r1"></a>

### 1 (§1): Attho akkharasaññāto (Meaning is represented by `akkhara`) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§1 (1): Attho akkharasaññāto" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m1" description="Meaning is represented by `akkhara`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Buddhappiya keeps Kaccāyana's first sutta first. It is not an operating rule but the sentence that names the subject of the whole grammar (letters, and the words made of them) and its purpose (the understanding of the Buddha's word), and he places it before the `Saññāvidhāna`, the assignment of technical terms that begins at [2 (§2): Akkharāpādayo ekacattālīsaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r2); the badge `sīhagatika` marks a rule whose scope is everything that follows. His gloss widens Kaccāyana's `sabbavacanānamattho` to "the meaning of every word, worldly (`lokiya`) or supramundane (`lokuttara`)", names the typical fault — an unaspirated letter (`sithila`) read for an aspirated one (`dhanita`), terms he will define at [11 (§9): Parasamaññā payoge](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r11) — and adds that words (`pada`) themselves count as `akkhara`, being nothing but collections of letters, so that the sutta covers the whole of grammar and not only the alphabet. The verse then turns the statement into an exhortation: skill in letters is to be acquired from a teacher, with the five duties towards him. There is no option marker.

:::

> Yo koci lokiyalokuttarādibhedo vacanattho, so sabbo akkhareheva saññāyate. Sithiladhanitādiakkharavipattiyañhi atthassa dunnayatā hoti, tasmā akkharakosallaṃ bahūpakāraṃ buddhavacanesu, ettha padānipi akkharasannipātarūpattā akkharesveva saṅgayhanti.

Whatever meaning of a word there is, of the kinds worldly (`lokiya`), supramundane (`lokuttara`) and the rest — all of it is made known (`saññāyate`) only through letters (`akkhara`). For when a letter goes wrong — an unaspirated (`sithila`) letter for an aspirated (`dhanita`) one, and the like — the meaning becomes hard to make out; therefore skill in letters is of great help in the words of the Buddha. Here words (`pada`) too, since in form they are collections of letters, are included under "letters".

:::tip[Examples]

The `sithila`–`dhanita` fault Buddhappiya names is the confusion of a letter with its aspirate, which turns one word into another:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(gata) | gato | gone |
| 🚻①⨀(ghata) | ghataṃ | ghee |
| 🚹①⨀(kara) | karo | a hand; a doer |
| 🚹①⨀(khara) | kharo | rough, harsh |

:::

> Tasmā akkharakosallaṃ, sampādeyya hitatthiko; \
> Upaṭṭhahaṃ garuṃ sammā, uṭṭhānādīhi pañcahi.

Therefore one who seeks his own good should acquire skill in letters, \
attending his teacher rightly with the five [duties] that begin with rising [to greet him].

---

## Saññāvidhāna (Definitions)

> Tatthādo tāva saddalakkhaṇe vohāraviññāpanatthaṃ saññāvidhānamārabhīyate.

There, first of all, in order to make known the technical usage (`vohāra`) of the science of words (`saddalakkhaṇa`), the assignment of technical terms (`saññāvidhāna`) is begun.

---

<a name="r2"></a>

### 2 (§2): Akkharāpādayo ekacattālīsaṃ (41 akkhara starting with `a`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§2 (2): Akkharāpādayo ekacattālīsaṃ" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m2" description="41 akkhara starting with `a`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first of the ten definitions of the `Saññāvidhāna` (2–11), which follow Kaccāyana's §2–§9 in order except that Buddhappiya inserts the two `garu` rules of the Kibbidhānakappa, [6 (§602): Dumhi garu](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r6) and [7 (§603): Dīgho ca](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r7), after the long vowels. Kaccāyana's vutti only lists the forty-one letters; Buddhappiya adds everything a beginner needs to say the list: the etymology of `akkhara` ("they do not wear away"), the principle behind the order of the letters (place, organ and effort of articulation — `ṭhāna`, `karaṇa`, `payatana`), the six places with the letters born in each, the organ and the effort for each class, the reason vowels come before consonants (they are the support, `nissaya`, the consonants the supported, `nissita`), and the point of the number: the count of forty-one is a boundary, and a text that uses more letters — the extra vowels and sibilants of Sanskrit — is by that token not the Buddha's word. His last remark explains the sutta's `api` as a back-reference to the letters already spoken of in [1 (§1): Attho akkharasaññāto](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r1). There is no option marker.

:::

> Akkharā api ādayo ekacattālīsaṃ, te ca kho jinavacanānurūpā akārādayo niggahītantā ekacattālīsamattā vaṇṇā paccekaṃ akkharā nāma honti. Taṃ yathā – a[^1] ā i ī u ū e o, ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma, ya ra lava sa ha ḷa aṃ-iti akkharā.

[^1]: CST4 prints the list from `ā`, dropping the `a` that heads the forty-one letters; it is supplied from the sutta's own `akārādayo` and from the list at [3 (§3): Tatthodantā sarā aṭṭha](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r3).

The letters (`akkhara`) too, beginning with `a`, are forty-one: those forty-one sounds (`vaṇṇa`), suited to the Conqueror's word, from `a` to the `niggahīta`, are each called `akkhara`. Namely: a ā i ī u ū e o, ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma, ya ra la va sa ha ḷa, aṃ — these are the letters.

> Nakkharantīti akkharā, a ādi yesaṃ te ādayo. Akārādīnamanukkamo panesa ṭhānādikkamasannissito, tathā hi ṭhānakaraṇappayatanehi vaṇṇā jāyante, tattha cha ṭhānāni kaṇṭhatālumuddhadantaoṭṭhanāsikāvasena.

They are `akkhara` because they do not wear away (`na kharanti`); `ādayo` are those whose beginning is `a`. This sequence of `a` and the rest rests on the sequence of place [of articulation] and so on: for sounds are produced by place (`ṭhāna`), organ (`karaṇa`) and effort (`payatana`). Of these, the places are six: throat, palate, head, teeth, lips and nose.

> Tattha – a vaṇṇa ka vagga ha kārā kaṇṭhajā.

Of these: the `a`-vaṇṇa [`a` and `ā`], the `ka`-group and `ha` are born in the throat.

> I vaṇṇa ca vagga ya kārā tālujā. Ṭa vagga ra kāra ḷa kārā muddhajā. Ta vagga la kāra sa kārā dantajā. U vaṇṇa pa vaggā oṭṭhajā. E kāro kaṇṭhatālujo. O kāro kaṇṭhoṭṭhajo.

The `i`-vaṇṇa, the `ca`-group and `ya` are born at the palate. The `ṭa`-group, `ra` and `ḷa` are born at the head [the roof of the mouth]. The `ta`-group, `la` and `sa` are born at the teeth. The `u`-vaṇṇa and the `pa`-group are born at the lips. `e` is born at throat and palate. `o` is born at throat and lips.

> Va kāro dantoṭṭhajo.

`va` is born at teeth and lips.

> Niggahītaṃ nāsikaṭṭhānajaṃ.

The `niggahīta` is born at the place of the nose.

> Ṅa ña ṇa na mā sakaṭṭhānajā, nāsikaṭṭhānajā cāti.

`ṅa`, `ña`, `ṇa`, `na` and `ma` are born both at their own place and at the place of the nose.

:::tip[Examples]

The forty-one letters by place of articulation, as Buddhappiya sets them out:

| place (`ṭhāna`) | letters |
| --- | --- |
| throat (`kaṇṭha`) | a ā, ka kha ga gha ṅa, ha |
| palate (`tālu`) | i ī, ca cha ja jha ña, ya |
| head (`muddha`) | ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ra, ḷa |
| teeth (`danta`) | ta tha da dha na, la, sa |
| lips (`oṭṭha`) | u ū, pa pha ba bha ma |
| throat and palate | e |
| throat and lips | o |
| teeth and lips | va |
| nose (`nāsikā`) | aṃ; and ṅa ña ṇa na ma, which are also born at their own place |

:::

> Ha kāraṃ pañcameheva, antaṭṭhāhi ca saṃyutaṃ; \
> Orasanti vadantettha, kaṇṭhajaṃ tadasaṃyutaṃ.

`ha` joined with the fifth [letter of a group] or with a semivowel (`antaṭṭha`) \
they here call chest-born (`orasa`); unjoined, it is throat-born.

> Karaṇaṃ jivhāmajjhaṃ tālujānaṃ, jivhopaggaṃ muddhajānaṃ, jivhāggaṃ dantajānaṃ, sesā sakaṭṭhānakaraṇā.

The organ (`karaṇa`) is the middle of the tongue for those born at the palate, the part near the tip of the tongue for those born at the head, the tip of the tongue for those born at the teeth; the rest have their own place as organ.

> Payatanaṃ saṃvutādikaraṇaviseso. Saṃvutattama kārassa, vivaṭattaṃ sesasarānaṃ sa kāraha kārānañca, phuṭṭhaṃ vaggānaṃ, īsaṃphuṭṭhaṃ yaralavānanti.

Effort (`payatana`) is the particular manner of the organ: closed (`saṃvuta`) and so on. Closure belongs to `a`; openness (`vivaṭa`) to the other vowels and to `sa` and `ha`; contact (`phuṭṭha`) to the group letters; slight contact (`īsaṃphuṭṭha`) to `ya`, `ra`, `la` and `va`.

> Evaṃ ṭhānakaraṇappayatanasutikālabhinnesu akkharesu sarā nissayā, itare nissitā. Tattha –

Thus, among the letters, which differ in place, organ, effort, sound (`suti`) and duration (`kāla`), the vowels are the support (`nissaya`) and the others the supported (`nissita`). On this –

> Nissayādo sarā vuttā, byañjanā nissitā tato; \
> Vaggekajā bahuttādo, tato ṭhānalahukkamā.

The vowels are stated first, as the support, and then the consonants, the supported; \
the group letters and then the single-born, for their greater number and so on; and thereafter in the order of place and of lightness.

> Vuttañca –

And it is said:

> ‘‘Pañcannaṃ pana ṭhānānaṃ, paṭipāṭivasāpi ca; \
> Nissayādippabhedehi, vutto tesamanukkamo’’ti.

"And by way of the sequence of the five places too, \
and by the distinctions of support and the rest, their order has been stated."

> Ekenādhikā cattālīsaṃ ekacattālīsaṃ, etena gaṇanaparicchedena –

Forty exceeded by one is forty-one; by this delimitation of the number –

> Adhikakkharavantāni, ekatālīsato ito; \
> Na buddhavacanānīti, dīpetācariyāsabho.

[Texts] that have more letters than these forty-one \
are not the word of the Buddha — so the bull among teachers makes known.

> Api ggahaṇaṃ heṭṭhā vuttānaṃ apekkhākaraṇatthaṃ.

The inclusion of `api` ("too") is for the purpose of referring back to those [letters] spoken of before [in the first sutta].

---

<a name="r3"></a>

### 3 (§3): Tatthodantā sarā aṭṭha ([From `akkhara`] eight ending with `o`: `sara`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§3 (3): Tatthodantā sarā aṭṭha" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m3" description="[From `akkhara`] eight ending with `o`: `sara`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second definition, in Kaccāyana's place: the letters have been listed, and now the first eight of them are named `sara`. Buddhappiya's gloss adds what the Kaccāyana vutti leaves implicit — the analysis of `odantā` as "those whose end is `o`", with the note that its `d` is a sandhi augment (the `d` of [34 (§35): Yava ma da na ta ra lā cāgamā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r34)), and two etymologies of `sara`: the vowels "go" (`saranti`) by themselves, or they "make the consonants go" (`sārenti`), which is the `nissaya`–`nissita` relation of the previous sutta stated as a name. He closes by marking the `anuvutti`: `tattha` ("among these") is carried into the next two definitions. There is no option marker.

:::

> Tattha tesu akkharesu a kārādīsu o kārantā aṭṭha akkharā sarā nāma honti. Taṃ yathā – a ā i ī u ū e o-iti sarā.

There, among those letters beginning with `a`, the eight letters that end with `o` are called `sara` (vowels). Namely: a ā i ī u ū e o — these are the vowels.

:::tip[Examples]

| `sara` | measure | `rassa` \| `dīgha` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a | one | short |
| ā | two | long |
| i | one | short |
| ī | two | long |
| u | one | short |
| ū | two | long |
| e | two | long |
| o | two | long |

:::

> O anto yesaṃ te odantā, da kāro sandhijo, saranti gacchantīti sarā, byañjane sārentītipi sarā.

Those whose end is `o` are `odantā`; the `d` [in `odantā`] arises from sandhi. They are `sara` because they go (`saranti`), that is, proceed [on their own]; or again `sara` because they make the consonants proceed (`sārenti`).

> ‘‘Tatthā’’ti vattate.

`Tattha` ("among these") continues [into the following rules].

---

<a name="r4"></a>

### 4 (§4): Lahumattā tayo rassā (3 short vowels: `rassa`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§4 (4): Lahumattā tayo rassā" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m4" description="3 short vowels: `rassa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Third definition, again in Kaccāyana's order, with `tattha` carried from [3 (§3): Tatthodantā sarā aṭṭha](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r3) so that "among the eight vowels" need not be said. Kaccāyana's vutti merely names `a`, `i`, `u`; Buddhappiya defines the measure (`mattā`) by which they are short — the time of a snap of the fingers or a blink of the eye — and states the whole scale at once: short vowels one measure, long vowels two, consonants half a measure (the `aḍḍhamattā` he will use again at [8 (§6): Sesā byañjanā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r8)). He also explains why the sutta says `lahumattā` ("of light measure") rather than `ekamattā` ("of one measure"): so that in verse a vowel drawn out to one and a half measures still counts as short. Two etymologies of `rassa` close the gloss, and `sara` and `rassa` are carried on into the next rule. There is no option marker.

:::

> Tattha aṭṭhasu saresu lahumattā tayo sarā rassā nāma honti. Taṃ yathā – a i u-iti rassā.

There, among the eight vowels, the three vowels of light measure are called `rassa` (short). Namely: a i u — these are the short vowels.

:::tip[Examples]

| `rassa` | measure | as in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a | one | 🚹①⨀(nara) naro, a man |
| i | one | 🚹①⨀(aggi) aggi, fire |
| u | one | 🚹①⨀(bhikkhu) bhikkhu, a monk |

:::

> Lahukā mattā pamāṇaṃ yesaṃ te lahumattā, mattā saddo cettha accharāsaṅghātaakkhinimīlanasaṅkhātaṃ kālaṃ vadati, tāya mattāya e kamattā rassā, dvimattā dīghā, aḍḍhamattā byañjanā. Lahu ggahaṇañcettha chandasi diyaḍḍhamattassāpi gahaṇatthaṃ. Rassakālayogato rassā, rassakālavanto vā rassā.

Those whose measure (`mattā`), their quantity, is light are `lahumattā`. The word `mattā` here denotes the time reckoned as a snap of the fingers or a blink of the eye; by that measure the short vowels have one measure, the long ones two measures, the consonants half a measure. And the word `lahu` ("light") is used here so that in verse (`chandasi`) a [vowel] of even one and a half measures may be included [as short]. They are `rassa` because of their connection with a short time, or `rassa` because they possess a short time.

> Sararassa ggahaṇāni ca vattante.

The words `sara` and `rassa` also continue.

---

<a name="r5"></a>

### 5 (§5): Aññe dīghā (Other [`sarā`] are `dīgha`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§5 (5): Aññe dīghā" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m5" description="Other [`sarā`] are `dīgha`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last of the vowel definitions, completing the pair with [4 (§4): Lahumattā tayo rassā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r4), whose `sara` and `rassa` it takes over ("other than the short ones"). Buddhappiya explains the choice of `aññe` ("others") over `dvimattā` ("of two measures") exactly as he explained `lahu`: a vowel drawn to one and a half measures in verse must count as long as well as short, and only a relative term admits it. Then comes an observation that has no counterpart in Kaccāyana: in some places `e` and `o` before a conjunct consonant are pronounced as if short (`ettha`, `seyyo`, `oṭṭho`, `sotthi`). The `kvaci` here is Buddhappiya's own, not the sutta's, and he uses it as Ruiz-Falqués describes the marker (*Boundaries and Domains* §4.2): to isolate an exception from the general state of affairs — for in `maṃ ce tvaṃ nikhaṇaṃ vane` and `putto tyāhaṃ mahārāja` the same vowels before a conjunct keep their full length, as the metre requires. The sutta itself has no option marker.

:::

> Tattha aṭṭhasu saresu rassehi aññe dvimattā pañca sarā dīghā nāma honti. Taṃ yathā – ā ī ū e o-iti dīghā.

There, among the eight vowels, the five vowels other than the short ones, having two measures, are called `dīgha` (long). Namely: ā ī ū e o — these are the long vowels.

> Añña ggahaṇaṃ diyaḍḍhamattikānampi saṅgahaṇatthaṃ. Dīghakāle niyuttā, tabbanto vā dīghā. Kvaci saṃyogapubbā ekārokārā rassā iva vuccante. Yathā – ettha, seyyo, oṭṭho, sotthi.

The word `añña` ("other") is used so as to include also [vowels] of one and a half measures. They are `dīgha` because they are set in a long time, or because they possess it. In some places (`kvaci`) `e` and `o` standing before a conjunct are pronounced as if they were short. For example: `ettha`, `seyyo`, `oṭṭho`, `sotthi`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(ettha) | ettha | here — `e` before `tth` |
| 🚹①⨀(seyya) | seyyo | better — `e` before `yy` |
| 🚹①⨀(oṭṭha) | oṭṭho | a lip; a camel — `o` before `ṭṭh` |
| 🚺①⨀(sotthi) | sotthi | safety — `o` before `tth` |

:::

> Kvacī ti kiṃ? Maṃ ce tvaṃ nikhaṇaṃ vane. Putto tyāhaṃ mahārāja.

Why "in some places"? [Because in] `maṃ ce tvaṃ nikhaṇaṃ vane` and `putto tyāhaṃ mahārāja` [the `e` and `o` before a conjunct remain long].

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `kvaci` (in some places) the shortening of `e` and `o` before a conjunct is not general: in these verses the `e` of `ce` before `tv` and the `o` of `putto` before `ty` keep their two measures.

> `mañce tvaṃ nikhaṇaṃ vane` \
> [(23J 22.1.1: Mūgapakkhajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.1#1319) \
⚧️②⨀(amha) 🔼(ce) ⚧️①⨀(tumha) ⏯🤘⨀(nikhaṇati) 🚻⑦⨀(vana) \
me | if | you | should bury | in the forest

> `putto tyāhaṃ mahārāja` \
> [(22J 1.1.7: Kaṭṭhahārijātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/1/1.1/1.1.7#14) \
🚹①⨀(putta) ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) ⚧️①⨀(amha) 🚹⓪⨀(mahārāja) \
son | your | I [am] | great king

The canon writes the first phrase `mañce`, with the `niggahīta` of `maṃ` assimilated to `c`.

:::

---

<a name="r6"></a>

### 6 (§602): Dumhi garu (The syllable before a conjunct is `garu`, heavy) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§602 (6): Dumhi garu" href="/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m602" description="The syllable before a conjunct is `garu`, heavy" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Buddhappiya lifts the two rules on the weight of syllables out of Kaccāyana's Kibbidhānakappa — §602 and §603 stand there to account for the short vowel of `datvā` and `hutvā` — and sets them among the definitions, directly after the long vowels of [5 (§5): Aññe dīghā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r5), because `garu` and `lahu` are properties of letters and the sandhi rules that invoke the metre (`chandānurakkhaṇa`, from [34 (§35): Yava ma da na ta ra lā cāgamā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r34) onward) presuppose them. The Bālāvatāra (¶45) follows him and states the whole doctrine in a line. His gloss explains the sutta's odd word: `du` is "a group of two", so `dumhi` means "before a conjunct letter" (`saṃyoga`, defined at [11 (§9): Parasamaññā payoge](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r11)), and the rule assigns the designation `garu` to the short letter that precedes it. `garu` is carried into the next rule. There is no option marker.

:::

> Dvinnaṃ samūho du, tasmiṃ dumhi. Saṃyogabhūte akkhare pare yo pubbo rassakkharo, so garusañño hoti. Yathā – datvā, hitvā, bhutvā.

`du` is a group of two; `dumhi` means "before such a [pair]". The short letter that stands before a letter that is a conjunct (`saṃyoga`) receives the designation `garu` (heavy). For example: `datvā`, `hitvā`, `bhutvā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔽(√dā + tvā) | datvā | having given — short `da` heavy before `tv` |
| 🔽(√hā + tvā) | hitvā | having abandoned — short `hi` heavy before `tv` |
| 🔽(√bhuja + tvā) | bhutvā | having eaten — short `bhu` heavy before `tv` |

:::

> ‘‘Garū’’ti vattate.

`garu` continues [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r7"></a>

### 7 (§603): Dīgho ca (And a long vowel is `garu`, heavy) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§603 (7): Dīgho ca" href="/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m603" description="And a long vowel is `garu`, heavy" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The companion of [6 (§602): Dumhi garu](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r6), with `garu` carried over: by saying `ca` the rule extends the designation to a second class, the syllable whose vowel is long, whatever follows it. Buddhappiya's five examples run through the five long vowels in the order of [5 (§5): Aññe dīghā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r5) — `ā` in `nāvā`, `ī` in `nadī`, `ū` in `vadhū`, `e` in `dve`, `o` in `tayo` — and he adds the definition Kaccāyana leaves to be inferred: every other syllable, a short vowel not before a conjunct, is `lahu` (light). `ca` here is an extension, not an option marker.

:::

> Dīgho ca saro garusañño hoti. Yathā – nāvā, nadī, vadhū, dve, tayo. Garukato añño ‘‘lahuko’’ti veditabbo.

And a long vowel receives the designation `garu`. For example: `nāvā`, `nadī`, `vadhū`, `dve`, `tayo`. [A syllable] other than a heavy one is to be understood as `lahuka` (light).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺①⨀(nāvā) | nāvā | a boat — `ā` heavy |
| 🚺①⨀(nadī) | nadī | a river — `ī` heavy |
| 🚺①⨀(vadhū) | vadhū | a young woman — `ū` heavy |
| ⚧️①⨂(dvi) | dve | two — `e` heavy |
| 🚹①⨂(ti) | tayo | three — `o` heavy; `ta` before it light |

:::

---

<a name="r8"></a>

### 8 (§6): Sesā byañjanā (Remaining are `byañjanā`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§6 (8): Sesā byañjanā" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m6" description="Remaining are `byañjanā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Back in Kaccāyana's sequence: the vowels being defined, the remaining letters are named `byañjana`. Where Kaccāyana's vutti lists them, Buddhappiya also counts them (thirty-three), gives their measure (half a `mattā`, on the scale of [4 (§4): Lahumattā tayo rassā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r4)), glosses `sesā` ("other than those already stated"), derives the name — they make meaning manifest, `byañjīyati` — and points out that the `a` in `ka`, `kha` and the rest is only a carrier for pronunciation: the consonant proper is the vowelless `k`, which is what [12 (§10): Pubbamadhoṭhitamassaraṃ sarena viyojaye](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r12) will detach. `byañjanā` continues into the next rule. There is no option marker.

:::

> Ṭhapetvā aṭṭha sare sesā aḍḍhamattā akkharā ka kārādayo niggahītantā tettiṃsa byañjanā nāma honti. Vuttehi aññe sesā. Byañjīyati etehi atthoti byañjanā. Taṃ yathā – ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma, ya ra lava sa ha ḷa aṃ-iti byañjanā. Ka kārādīsvakāro uccāraṇattho.

Setting aside the eight vowels, the remaining letters of half a measure, from `ka` to the `niggahīta`, thirty-three [in number], are called `byañjana` (consonants). "Remaining" means other than those already stated. They are `byañjana` because meaning is made manifest (`byañjīyati`) by them. Namely: ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma, ya ra la va sa ha ḷa, aṃ — these are the consonants. The `a` in `ka` and the rest is for the sake of pronunciation.

:::tip[Examples]

| class | letters | count |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the five groups (`vagga`) | ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma | 25 |
| the ungrouped (`avagga`) | ya ra la va sa ha ḷa | 7 |
| the `niggahīta` | aṃ | 1 |

:::

> ‘‘Byañjanā’’ti vattate.

`byañjanā` continues.

---

<a name="r9"></a>

### 9 (§7): Vaggā pañcapañcaso mantā (`vagga`: 5 by 5 [byañjane] ending with `ma`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§7 (9): Vaggā pañcapañcaso mantā" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m7" description="`vagga`: 5 by 5 [byañjane] ending with `ma`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The definition of the groups, with `byañjanā` carried from [8 (§6): Sesā byañjanā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r8). The rule statement is Kaccāyana's; Buddhappiya adds the names in use — the `ka`-group, the `ca`-group and so on, after the first letter of each — a gloss of `vagga` as "collection" (`samūha`), and two analyses of the sutta's `pañcapañcaso`: "by a division of five each" or "having five each". `mantā` he analyses as "those whose end is `ma`", which fixes the twenty-five as the letters from `ka` to `ma` and leaves `ya` and the rest outside the groups. There is no option marker.

:::

> Tesaṃ kho byañjanānaṃ ka kārādayo ma kārantā pañcavīsati byañjanā pañcapañcavibhāgena vaggā nāma honti. Taṃ yathā – ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma-iti vaggā.

Of those consonants, the twenty-five from `ka` to `ma`, divided five by five, are called `vagga` (groups). Namely: ka kha ga gha ṅa, ca cha ja jha ña, ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa, ta tha da dha na, pa pha ba bha ma — these are the groups.

:::tip[Examples]

| group | first | second | third | fourth | fifth |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ka`-vagga | ka | kha | ga | gha | ṅa |
| `ca`-vagga | ca | cha | ja | jha | ña |
| `ṭa`-vagga | ṭa | ṭha | ḍa | ḍha | ṇa |
| `ta`-vagga | ta | tha | da | dha | na |
| `pa`-vagga | pa | pha | ba | bha | ma |

:::

> Te pana paṭhamakkharavasena ka vaggaca vaggādivohāraṃ gatā, vaggo ti samūho, tattha pañcapañcavibhāgenāti vā pañca pañca etesamatthīti vā pañcapañcaso, mo anto yesaṃ te mantā.

These have come to be called, after their first letter, the `ka`-group, the `ca`-group and so on. `vagga` means a collection. In [the sutta], `pañcapañcaso` means either "by a division of five each" or "those which have five each"; `mantā` are those whose end is `ma`.

---

<a name="r10"></a>

### 10 (§8): Aṃiti niggahītaṃ (`aṃ` represents `niggahita`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§8 (10): Aṃ iti niggahitaṃ" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m8" description="`aṃ` represents `niggahita`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last letter to be named, in Kaccāyana's place. Kaccāyana's vutti simply equates `aṃ` with `niggahīta`; Buddhappiya explains how the sutta manages to name a sound that cannot be pronounced alone: the `a` is a carrier for pronunciation, as in `ka` at [8 (§6): Sesā byañjanā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r8), and `iti` points to what has just been uttered, so that the name attaches to the dot (`bindu`) after the `a`. He then gives two etymologies from `gayhati` "is pronounced" — pronounced in dependence on a short vowel (it never follows a long one, a fact he will use at [57 (§37): Niggahitañca](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r57)), or pronounced with the organ of articulation "checked" (`niggahetvā`) — and fixes the second in a verse. The closing sentence introduces the next rule as a `paribhāsā`. There is no option marker.

:::

> A kāro uccāraṇattho, iti saddo panānantaravuttanidassanattho, aṃiti yaṃ a kārato paraṃ vuttaṃ bindu, taṃ niggahītaṃ nāma hoti. Rassassaraṃ nissāya gayhati, karaṇaṃ niggahetvā gayhatīti vā niggahītaṃ.

The `a` is for pronunciation; the word `iti` serves to point to what has just been uttered: the dot (`bindu`) that is uttered after the `a` in `aṃ` is called `niggahīta`. It is `niggahīta` because it is pronounced (`gayhati`) in dependence on a short vowel, or because it is pronounced with the organ [of articulation] checked (`niggahetvā`).

:::tip[Examples]

The `niggahīta` rides on each of the three short vowels:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(buddha) | buddhaṃ | the Buddha — `aṃ` |
| 🚹②⨀(aggi) | aggiṃ | the fire — `iṃ` |
| 🚹②⨀(bhikkhu) | bhikkhuṃ | the monk — `uṃ` |

:::

> Karaṇaṃ niggahetvāna, mukhenāvivaṭena yaṃ; \
> Vuccate niggahītanti, vuttaṃ bindu sarānugaṃ.

That which is spoken with the organ [of articulation] checked and with the mouth not opened \
is called `niggahīta`: the dot pronounced in the wake of a vowel.

> Idha avuttānaṃ parasamaññānampi payojane sati gahaṇatthaṃ paribhāsamāha.

Now, so that the technical terms of other [grammars] that are not stated here may also be taken up when there is a use for them, he states a `paribhāsā` (an interpretive rule).

---

<a name="r11"></a>

### 11 (§9): Parasamaññā payoge (Other grammatical terms when applicable) :badge[saññaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§9 (11): Parasamaññā payoge" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m9" description="Other grammatical terms when applicable" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The rule that closes the `Saññāvidhāna`, where Kaccāyana also has it: the terms the grammar has not defined itself may be borrowed from "others" when there is a use for them (`payoge sati`). Kaccāyana's vutti names the Sanskrit treatise and admits two terms, `ghosa` and `aghosa`, which [42 (§29): Vagge ghosāghosānaṃ tatiyapaṭhamā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r42) will need. Buddhappiya widens the source to "other grammarians" and turns the rule into a glossary of everything his own commentary has used or will use: `ghosa` and `aghosa` with their counts; `dhanita` and `sithila`, already used at [1 (§1): Attho akkharasaññāto](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r1); `lopa`, the operation of [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13); `savaṇṇa`, equated with `sarūpa`, the words of [15 (§13): Vā paro asarūpā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r15) and [16 (§14): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r16); `saṃyoga`, used at [6 (§602): Dumhi garu](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r6); and `liṅga` and `pada`, the stem and the inflected word, which [19 (§17): Yamedantassādeso](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r19) (`padanta`) and the verse at [29 (§58): Ivaṇṇuvaṇṇā jhalā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r29) presuppose. `payoge` is the only condition; there is no option marker.

:::

> Yā ca pana parasmiṃ sakkataganthe, paresaṃ vā veyyākaraṇānaṃ samaññā ghosāghosalopasavaṇṇasaṃyogaliṅgādikā, tā payoge sati etthāpi yujjante.

And whatever technical terms (`samaññā`) there are in another [treatise], the Sanskrit book, or belonging to other grammarians — `ghosa`, `aghosa`, `lopa`, `savaṇṇa`, `saṃyoga`, `liṅga` and the rest — these are applied here too when there is a use for them.

> Parasmiṃ, paresaṃ vā samaññā parasamaññā, veyyākaraṇe, veyyākaraṇamadhītānaṃ vā samaññātyattho. Payujjanaṃ payogo, viniyogo.

`parasamaññā` are terms in another [work], or terms of others — that is, terms in a grammar, or of those who have studied grammar. `payoga` is applying, employment.

> Tattha vaggānaṃ paṭhamadutiyā, sa kāro ca aghosā. Vaggānaṃ tatiyacatutthapañcamā, ya ra lava ha ḷā cāti ekavīsati ghosā nāma.

Among these, the first and second [letters] of the groups, and `sa`, are `aghosa` (unvoiced). The third, fourth and fifth of the groups, and `ya ra la va ha ḷa` — twenty-one — are called `ghosa` (voiced).

> Ettha ca vaggānaṃ dutiyacatutthā dhanitā tipi vuccanti, itare sithilā ti. Vināso lopo. Rassassarā sakadīghehi aññamaññaṃ savaṇṇā nāma, ye sarūpā tipi vuccanti. Sarānantaritāni byañjanāni saṃyogo. Dhātuppaccayavibhattivajjitamatthavaṃ liṅgaṃ. Vibhatyantaṃ padaṃ. Iccevamādi.

And here the second and fourth of the groups are also called `dhanita` (aspirated), the others `sithila` (unaspirated). `lopa` (elision) is disappearance. The short vowels together with their own long ones are mutually `savaṇṇa` (homogeneous), and these are also called `sarūpa` (of the same form). Consonants not separated by a vowel are a `saṃyoga` (conjunct). A meaningful [element] other than a root, a suffix or an inflection is a `liṅga` (stem). What ends in an inflection is a `pada` (word). And so on.

:::tip[Examples]

The borrowed terms as Buddhappiya defines them:

| term | definition | e.g. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `aghosa` (unvoiced) | first and second of each group, and `sa` | ka kha, ca cha, ṭa ṭha, ta tha, pa pha, sa — eleven |
| `ghosa` (voiced) | third, fourth and fifth of each group, and ya ra la va ha ḷa | ga gha ṅa … ba bha ma, ya ra la va ha ḷa — twenty-one |
| `dhanita` (aspirated) | second and fourth of each group | kha gha, cha jha, ṭha ḍha, tha dha, pha bha |
| `sithila` (unaspirated) | the other group letters | ka ga ṅa, ca ja ña, … |
| `lopa` (elision) | disappearance of a letter | `lok~~a~~ + aggapuggalo` at [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13) |
| `savaṇṇa` = `sarūpa` (homogeneous) | a short vowel and its own long | a–ā, i–ī, u–ū |
| `saṃyoga` (conjunct) | consonants with no vowel between them | `tv` in `datvā`, `tth` in `ettha` |
| `liṅga` (stem) | a meaningful element that is not a root, suffix or inflection | `purisa` in `puriso` |
| `pada` (word) | what ends in an inflection | `puriso` |

:::

---

> _Iti saññāvidhānaṃ._

Thus the assignment of technical terms.

---

## Sarasandhividhāna (Vowel sandhi)

> Atha sarasandhi vuccate.

Now vowel sandhi (`sarasandhi`) is stated.

> Loka aggapuggalo, paññā indriyaṃ, tīṇi imāni, no hi etaṃ, bhikkhunī ovādo, mātu upaṭṭhānaṃ, sametu āyasmā, abhibhū āyatanaṃ, dhanā me atthi, sabbe eva, tayo assu dhammā, asanto ettha na dissanti itīdha sarādisaññāyaṃ sabbasandhikaraṇaṭṭhāne byañjanaviyojanatthaṃ paribhāsamāha.

Here, in `loka aggapuggalo`, `paññā indriyaṃ`, `tīṇi imāni`, `no hi etaṃ`, `bhikkhunī ovādo`, `mātu upaṭṭhānaṃ`, `sametu āyasmā`, `abhibhū āyatanaṃ`, `dhanā me atthi`, `sabbe eva`, `tayo assu dhammā` and `asanto ettha na dissanti`, now that the designations `sara` and the rest are known, he states a `paribhāsā` for the purpose of separating the consonant [from its vowel] at every place where sandhi is to be made.

---

<a name="r12"></a>

### 12 (§10): Pubbamadhoṭhitamassaraṃ sarena viyojaye (In preceeding [word], detach vowel from vowelless [consonant]) :badge[vidhyaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§10 (12): Pubbamadhoṭhita massaraṃ sarena viyojaye" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m10" description="In preceeding [word], detach vowel from vowelless [consonant]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Buddhappiya opens the `Sarasandhividhāna` with Kaccāyana's first procedural rule but changes the order of the next two: Kaccāyana has separate (§10), rejoin (§11), elide (§12); the Rūpasiddhi has separate (12), elide (13), rejoin (14), so that the two `paribhāsā` frame the first operation in the order in which the work is actually done, and every derivation in the book runs §10, the operating rule, §11. The twelve phrases quoted in the opening sentence are the material: they are separated here, elided at [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13) and rejoined at [14 (§11): Naye paraṃ yutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r14). His gloss reads the instrumental `sarena` in two ways — as a `nissakka`, "[apart] from the vowel", or "together with its vowel" — and settles what the sutta never names: the thing detached is a consonant, which follows from `assaraṃ`, since only a consonant can be made "vowelless". Where Kaccāyana's vutti says `sandhiṃ kattukāmo` ("wishing to make sandhi"), his `anatikkamanto` adds that it is the consonant next to the junction, and no other, that is taken. There is no option marker: this is an instruction (`vidhyaṅga`), not an operation.

:::

> Sarenā ti nissakke karaṇavacanaṃ, sahayoge vā, sandhitabbe sarasahitaṃ pubbabyañjanaṃ anatikkamanto adhoṭhitamassarañca katvā sarato viyojayeti sarato byañjanaṃ viyojetabbaṃ. Ettha ca assara ggahaṇasāmatthiyena ‘‘byañjana’’nti laddhaṃ.

`sarena` is an instrumental form ③ used in the sense of separation (`nissakka`, "from the vowel"), or else in the sense of accompaniment ("with its vowel"). In [a pair of words] to be joined, not passing over the preceding consonant that carries its vowel, one should make it stand below (`adhoṭhita`) and vowelless (`assara`) and separate it from the vowel — that is, the consonant is to be detached from the vowel. And here, by the force of the word `assara` ("vowelless"), "consonant" is obtained [as the thing detached, though the sutta does not name it].

:::tip[Examples]

The twelve phrases of the section's opening, with the final consonant of the first word detached from its vowel:

| words to be joined | separated by §10 |
| --- | --- |
| loka aggapuggalo | lok + a + aggapuggalo |
| paññā indriyaṃ | paññ + ā + indriyaṃ |
| tīṇi imāni | tīṇ + i + imāni |
| no hi etaṃ | no h + i + etaṃ |
| bhikkhunī ovādo | bhikkhun + ī + ovādo |
| mātu upaṭṭhānaṃ | māt + u + upaṭṭhānaṃ |
| sametu āyasmā | samet + u + āyasmā |
| abhibhū āyatanaṃ | abhibh + ū + āyatanaṃ |
| dhanā me atthi | dhanā m + e + atthi |
| sabbe eva | sabb + e + eva |
| tayo assu dhammā | tay + o + assu dhammā |
| asanto ettha na dissanti | asant + o + ettha na dissanti |

:::

---

<a name="r13"></a>

### 13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ (Vowel, before vowel, is elided) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12" description="Vowel, before vowel, is elided" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The general rule (`utsarga`) of vowel sandhi, placed between the two `paribhāsā`: a vowel before a vowel drops. Kaccāyana's vutti states it in a line and gives examples; Buddhappiya's gloss is a lesson in how a sutta is read. `sarā` names the thing operated on (`kāriyī`), `sare` the condition (`nimitta`), `lopaṃ` the operation (`kāriya`). The plural `sarā` tells us that before any one following vowel all sorts of vowels drop. The locative `sare` is a `nimittasattamī`, a locative of condition, and because the following vowel is the condition, no sandhi takes place when a letter or a pause (`vaṇṇakālabyavadhāna`) intervenes between the two. Most important for the rules that follow: a locative condition operates on what precedes it, not on what follows — so this rule elides the preceding vowel (`pubbalopa`), and [15 (§13): Vā paro asarūpā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r15), which needs the word `paro` to elide the following one (`paralopa`), is the exception to it. There is no option marker: outside the exceptions, the elision is obligatory. The closing sentence marks `assaraṃ` and `adhoṭhitaṃ` as carried from [12 (§10): Pubbamadhoṭhitamassaraṃ sarena viyojaye](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r12) into the next `paribhāsā`, "for smooth utterance".

:::

> Sarā kho sabbepi sare pare ṭhite lopaṃ papponti. Lopo ti adassanaṃ anuccāraṇaṃ. Ettha sarā ti kāriyīniddeso. Bahuvacanaṃ panettha ekekasmiṃ sare pare bahūnaṃ lopañāpanatthaṃ. Sare ti nimittaniddeso, nimittasattamī cāyaṃ, nimittopādānasāmatthiyato vaṇṇakālabyavadhāne sandhikāriyaṃ na hoti. Lopa nti kāriyaniddeso, idaṃ pana suttaṃ upari paralopavidhānato pubbalopavidhānanti daṭṭhabbaṃ, evaṃ sabbattha sattamīniddese pubbasseva vidhi, na parassa vidhānanti veditabbaṃ.

Vowels, all of them, when a vowel stands next, undergo elision (`lopa`). `lopa` is non-appearance, non-pronunciation. Here `sarā` designates the thing operated on (`kāriyī`). The plural is used here so as to make known that before each single following vowel many [different] vowels are elided. `sare` designates the condition (`nimitta`), and this is a locative ⑦ of condition (`nimittasattamī`); by the force of taking [the following vowel] as the condition, the sandhi operation does not take place when a letter or a pause (`vaṇṇa-kāla-byavadhāna`) intervenes. `lopaṃ` designates the operation (`kāriya`). This sutta, moreover, is to be seen as prescribing the elision of the preceding [vowel], because the elision of the following one is prescribed further on; and so it is to be understood that everywhere, when the designation is in the locative, the operation is on the preceding [element] alone, and no operation is prescribed for the following one.

:::tip[Examples]

The elision applied to the phrases separated at [12 (§10): Pubbamadhoṭhitamassaraṃ sarena viyojaye](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r12); the rejoining is the work of the next rule, where the results are listed.

|     | loka + aggapuggalo              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | lok + (a) + (a) + ggapuggalo    | §10  |
| →   | lok + (~~a~~ + a) + ggapuggalo  | §12  |
| →   | (lok + a) + ggapuggalo          | §11  |
| →   | **lokaggapuggalo**              |      |

|     | tayo + assu                | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tay + (o) + (a) + ssu      | §10  |
| →   | tay + (~~o~~ + a) + ssu    | §12  |
| →   | (tay + a) + ssu            | §11  |
| →   | **tayassu**                |      |

|     | dhanā me + atthi           | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dhanā m + (e) + (a) + tthi | §10  |
| →   | dhanā m + (~~e~~ + a) + tthi | §12 |
| →   | dhanā (m + a) + tthi       | §11  |
| →   | **dhanā matthi**           |      |

:::

> ‘‘Assaraṃ, adhoṭhita’’nti ca vattate, siliṭṭhakathane paribhāsamāha.

`assaraṃ` and `adhoṭhitaṃ` also continue; for smooth utterance (`siliṭṭhakathana`) he [now] states a `paribhāsā`.

---

<a name="r14"></a>

### 14 (§11): Naye paraṃ yutte (To conclude, attach next) :badge[vidhyaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§11 (14): Naye paraṃ yutte" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m11" description="To conclude, attach next" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second `paribhāsā`, moved after the elision so that it closes the procedure: the consonant left vowelless at [12 (§10): Pubbamadhoṭhitamassaraṃ sarena viyojaye](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r12) is carried to the letter that now follows it, `assaraṃ` and `adhoṭhitaṃ` being carried over from the two preceding rules. Buddhappiya explains the sutta's `yutte`, "where it is fitting": it excludes the `niggahīta`, which is never carried to a following vowel — Kaccāyana's vutti gives the same counter-example, `akkocchi maṃ avadhi maṃ`, without saying why. The twelve phrases of the section's opening are completed here, so that the examples of the first three rules form one set. The closing sentence introduces the sixteen phrases of the next rule and the device by which its wording is obtained: the `sare` of [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13) still governs, but by the maxim "the inflection changes according to the sense" (`atthavasā vibhattivipariṇāmo`) the `sarā … lopaṃ` of that rule is re-read as `saramhā` ⑤ ("after a vowel") … `saro` ① ("the vowel") … `lopaṃ`, which is what `Vā paro asarūpā` needs. There is no option marker.

:::

> Sararahitaṃ kho byañjanaṃ adhoṭhitaṃ parakkharaṃ naye yutte ṭhāneti paranayanaṃ kātabbaṃ. Ettha yutta ggahaṇaṃ niggahītanisedhanatthaṃ, tena ‘‘akkocchi maṃ avadhi ma’’ntiādīsu paranayanasandeho na hoti.

A consonant deprived of its vowel, standing below, one should carry to the following letter where it is fitting (`yutte`): that is, the carrying-over to the following (`paranayana`) is to be done. Here the word `yutta` serves to exclude the `niggahīta`; hence in `akkocchi maṃ avadhi maṃ` and the like there is no doubt about the carrying-over [the `ṃ` is not carried to the vowel].

> Lokaggapuggalo, paññindriyaṃ, tīṇimāni, nohetaṃ, bhikkhunovādo, mātupaṭṭhānaṃ, sametāyasmā, abhibhāyatanaṃ, dhanā matthi, sabbeva, tayassu dhammā, asantettha na dissanti.

The twelve phrases, joined:

:::tip[Examples]

| words | elided by §12 | carried over by §11 | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loka aggapuggalo | ~~a~~ | **lokaggapuggalo** | the foremost person in the world |
| paññā indriyaṃ | ~~ā~~ | **paññindriyaṃ** | the faculty of wisdom |
| tīṇi imāni | ~~i~~ | **tīṇimāni** | these three |
| no hi etaṃ | ~~i~~ | **nohetaṃ** | certainly not |
| bhikkhunī ovādo | ~~ī~~ | **bhikkhunovādo** | the exhortation of the nuns |
| mātu upaṭṭhānaṃ | ~~u~~ | **mātupaṭṭhānaṃ** | attending on one's mother |
| sametu āyasmā | ~~u~~ | **sametāyasmā** | let the venerable one agree |
| abhibhū āyatanaṃ | ~~ū~~ | **abhibhāyatanaṃ** | the base of mastery |
| dhanā me atthi | ~~e~~ | **dhanā matthi** | I have riches |
| sabbe eva | ~~e~~ | **sabbeva** | all indeed |
| tayo assu dhammā | ~~o~~ | **tayassu dhammā** | three things indeed |
| asanto ettha na dissanti | ~~o~~ | **asantettha na dissanti** | the bad are not seen here |

> `tayassu dhammā jahitā bhavanti` \
> [(18Kh 6: Ratanasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Kh/6#54) \
🚹①⨂(ti) 🔼(assu) 🚹①⨂(dhamma) 🚹①⨂(jahita) ▶️🤟⨂(bhavati) \
three | indeed | things | abandoned | are

> `asantettha na dissanti` \
> [(18Dh 21.8: Cūḷasubhaddāvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/21/21.8#327) \
🚹①⨂(asanta) 🔼(ettha) 🔼(na) 🔵▶️🤟⨂(dissati) \
the bad | here | not | are seen

> `sametāyasmā saṃghena` \
> [(1V 1.2.10: Saṃghabhedasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.10#1679) \
▶️🤟⨀(sameti) 🚹①⨀(āyasmantu) 🚹③⨀(saṃgha) \
let … be reconciled | the venerable one | with the Saṅgha

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `yutte` (where it is fitting) a `niggahīta` left standing before a vowel is not carried over to it: in `akkocchi maṃ avadhi maṃ` the `ṃ` of `maṃ` stays where it is.

> `akkocchi maṃ avadhi maṃ` \
> [(3V 10.2: Dīghāvuvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/10/10.2#1982) \
⏮🤟⨀(akkosati) ⚧️②⨀(amha) ⏮🤟⨀(vadhati) ⚧️②⨀(amha) \
he abused | me | he struck | me

:::

> Yassa idāni, saññā iti, chāyā iva, kathā eva kā, iti api, assamaṇī asi, cakkhu indriyaṃ, akataññū asi, ākāse iva, te api, vande ahaṃ, so ahaṃ, cattāro ime, vasalo iti, moggallāno āsi bījako, pāto evātīdha pubbalope sampatte ‘‘sare’’ti adhikāro, idha pana ‘‘atthavasā vibhattivipariṇāmo’’ti katvā ‘‘saro, saramhā, lopa’’nti ca vattamāne –

Here, in `yassa idāni`, `saññā iti`, `chāyā iva`, `kathā eva kā`, `iti api`, `assamaṇī asi`, `cakkhu indriyaṃ`, `akataññū asi`, `ākāse iva`, `te api`, `vande ahaṃ`, `so ahaṃ`, `cattāro ime`, `vasalo iti`, `moggallāno āsi bījako` and `pāto eva`, when the elision of the preceding vowel [by the last rule] is due, the governing word `sare` [still holds]; but here, applying [the maxim] "the inflection changes according to the sense" (`atthavasā vibhattivipariṇāmo`), while `saro` [①, "the vowel"], `saramhā` [⑤, "after a vowel"] and `lopaṃ` continue –

---

<a name="r15"></a>

### 15 (§13): Vā paro asarūpā (Optionally, next dissimilar [vowel]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§13 (15): Vā paro asarūpā" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m13" description="Optionally, next dissimilar [vowel]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first exception to [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13), in Kaccāyana's place: after a dissimilar vowel it is the following vowel that drops, and only optionally. Buddhappiya reads the sutta with the words of 13 re-inflected by the maxim quoted at the end of [14 (§11): Naye paraṃ yutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r14) — `saramhā` ⑤ `paro saro` ① `lopaṃ … vā`, "after a vowel, the following vowel [undergoes] elision, or [not]". Where Kaccāyana's vutti gives two examples and two counter-examples, he gives sixteen examples, ten places where the rule does not reach, and a summary verse; and this is the sutta in which he sets out his doctrine of `vā` (Ruiz-Falqués, *Boundaries and Domains* §3.2–§3.3). Like the prefix `ā`, which marks either an exclusive limit (`mariyādā`, "up to but not including") or an inclusive one (`abhividhi`, "up to and including"), `vā` has two uses: in some rules an open option (`vikappa`), in others the delimitation of forms as they are fixed in usage (`yathāvavatthitarūpapariggaha`) — the `vavatthitavibhāsā`, or restricted option. Here it is the latter, and the restricted option has three domains. `nicca`: the elision of the following vowel is the only sandhi possible — `cakkhu indriyaṃ` gives `cakkhundriyaṃ` and never `cakkhindriyaṃ`, Buddhappiya's own `niccaṃ`; Ruiz-Falqués reads `yassa idāni` and the other `idāni`, `iti`, `iva`, `eva` cases the same way, since `yassidāni` does not occur. `anicca`: the elision is one of two correct forms — `vasaloti` or `vasalo iti` (the canon has the latter), `moggallānosi` or `moggallāno āsi`. `asanta`, "inapplicable": the `idha na bhavati` list, where the following vowel never drops and the general rule 13 elides the preceding one instead (`pañcindriyāni`, never `pañcandriyāni`). The verse draws the boundary: after `a` or `ā` the following vowel is not elided except in `idāni`, `iti`, `iva`, `eva` and the like; after any other vowel a following long vowel is not elided, except in `āsi`, `eva` and the like. The last block carries `paro` and `asarūpe` into the next rule and, by a lion's glance (`sīhagati`), fetches the words `ivaṇṇa` from [21 (§21): Ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r21) and `u` from [20 (§18): Vamodudantānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r20) to serve as its subject.

:::

> Asamānarūpamhā saramhā paro saro lopaṃ pappoti vā. Samānaṃ rūpaṃ assāti sarūpo, na sarūpo asarūpo, asavaṇṇo. Yasmā pana mariyādāyaṃ, abhividhimhi ca vattamāno ā upasaggo viya vā saddo dvidhā vattate, katthaci vikappe, katthaci yathāvavatthitarūpapariggahe, idha pana pacchime, tato niccamaniccamasantañca vidhimettha vā saddo dīpeti. ‘‘Naye paraṃ yutte’’ti paraṃ netabbaṃ.

After a vowel of dissimilar form, the following vowel optionally (`vā`) undergoes elision. `sarūpa` is that which has the same form; `asarūpa` is what is not `sarūpa`, that is, not homogeneous (`asavaṇṇa`). Now, just as the prefix `ā`, which is used for an exclusive limit (`mariyādā`) and for an inclusive limit (`abhividhi`), so the word `vā` is used in two ways: in some places for an open option (`vikappa`), in some places for the delimitation of forms as they are fixed [in usage] (`yathāvavatthitarūpapariggaha`); and here it is the latter. Hence in this rule the word `vā` indicates an operation that is [at once] mandatory (`nicca`), non-mandatory (`anicca`) and inapplicable (`asanta`). By [14 (§11): Naye paraṃ yutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r14) the [consonant] is to be carried to the following.

> Yassadāni yassa idāni, saññāti saññā iti, chāyāva chāyā iva, kathāva kā kathā eva kā, itīpi iti api, assamaṇīsi assamaṇī asi. Cakkhundriya miti niccaṃ. Akataññūsi akataññū asi, ākāseva ākāse iva, tepi te api, vandehaṃ vande ahaṃ, sohaṃ so ahaṃ, cattārome cattāro ime, vasaloti vasalo iti, moggallānosi bījako moggallāno āsi bījako, pātova pāto eva.

`yassadāni` from `yassa idāni`, `saññāti` from `saññā iti`, `chāyāva` from `chāyā iva`, `kathāva kā` from `kathā eva kā`, `itīpi` from `iti api`, `assamaṇīsi` from `assamaṇī asi`. `cakkhundriyaṃ` [from `cakkhu indriyaṃ`] — this one always (`niccaṃ`). `akataññūsi` from `akataññū asi`, `ākāseva` from `ākāse iva`, `tepi` from `te api`, `vandehaṃ` from `vande ahaṃ`, `sohaṃ` from `so ahaṃ`, `cattārome` from `cattāro ime`, `vasaloti` from `vasalo iti`, `moggallānosi bījako` from `moggallāno āsi bījako`, `pātova` from `pāto eva`.

:::tip[Examples]

| words | following vowel elided | result | domain |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| yassa idāni | ~~i~~ | **yassadāni** | `idāni` after `a`: the verse's first exception |
| saññā iti | ~~i~~ | **saññāti** | `iti` after `ā` |
| chāyā iva | ~~i~~ | **chāyāva** | `iva` after `ā` |
| kathā eva kā | ~~e~~ | **kathāva kā** | `eva` after `ā` |
| iti api | ~~a~~ | **itīpi** | with `ī` by [18 (§16): Pubbo ca](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r18) |
| assamaṇī asi | ~~a~~ | **assamaṇīsi** | `anicca`: `assamaṇī asi` also stands |
| cakkhu indriyaṃ | ~~i~~ | **cakkhundriyaṃ** | `nicca`, Buddhappiya's own label |
| akataññū asi | ~~a~~ | **akataññūsi** | `anicca` |
| ākāse iva | ~~i~~ | **ākāseva** | `anicca` |
| te api | ~~a~~ | **tepi** | `anicca` |
| vande ahaṃ | ~~a~~ | **vandehaṃ** | `anicca` |
| so ahaṃ | ~~a~~ | **sohaṃ** | `anicca` |
| cattāro ime | ~~i~~ | **cattārome** | `anicca` |
| vasalo iti | ~~i~~ | **vasaloti** | `anicca`: the canon has `vasalo iti` |
| moggallāno āsi bījako | ~~ā~~ | **moggallānosi bījako** | `āsi`: the verse's second exception |
| pāto eva | ~~e~~ | **pātova** | `eva` after `o`: the second exception |

|     | yassa + idāni                | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | yass + (a) + (i) + dāni      | §10  |
| →   | yass + (a + ~~i~~) + dāni    | §13  |
| →   | (yass + a) + dāni            | §11  |
| →   | **yassadāni**                |      |

|     | cakkhu + indriyaṃ                 | rule |
| --- | --------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | cakkh + (u) + (i + n) + driyaṃ    | §10  |
| →   | cakkh + (u + ~~i~~) + n + driyaṃ  | §13  |
| →   | (cakkh + u + n) + driyaṃ          | §11  |
| →   | **cakkhundriyaṃ**                 |      |

|     | moggallāno + āsi                 | rule |
| --- | -------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | moggallān + (o) + (ā) + si       | §10  |
| →   | moggallān + (o + ~~ā~~) + si     | §13  |
| →   | (moggallān + o) + si             | §11  |
| →   | **moggallānosi**                 |      |

> `yassadāni` \
> [(1V 1.1.3: Tatiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.3#382) \
🚹⑥⨀(ya) 🔼(idāni) \
for whom | now

> `assamaṇīsi` \
> [(1V 1.1.2.2: Vinītavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2/1.1.2.2#303) \
🚺①⨀(assamaṇī) ▶️🤘⨀(atthi) \
no nun | you are

> `akataññūsi` \
> [(18Dh 26.1: Pasādabahulabrāhmaṇavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/26/26.1#411) \
🚹①⨀(akataññū) ▶️🤘⨀(atthi) \
a knower of the unmade | you are

> `taṃ jaññā vasalo iti` \
> [(18Sn 1.7: Vasalasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/1/1.7#136) \
🚹②⨀(ta) ⏯🤟⨀(jānāti) 🚹①⨀(vasala) 🔼(iti) \
him | one should know | an outcast | as

> `moggallānosi bījako` \
> [(23J 22.1.8: Mahānāradakassapajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.8#2676) \
🚹①⨀(moggallāna) ⏮🤟⨀(atthi) 🚹①⨀(bījaka) \
Moggallāna | was | Bījaka

> `pātova sannipatitvā` \
> [(20Ap1 1.3: Sāriputtattheraapadāna)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/20Ap1/1/1.3#197) \
🔼(pāto) 🔼(eva) 🔽(sannipatitvā) \
early in the morning | just | having assembled

The canon writes `vasalo iti` unjoined: the `anicca` domain, where both forms are correct.

:::

> Idha na bhavati – pañcindriyāni, saddhindriyaṃ, sattuttamo, ekūnavīsati, yassete, sugatovādo, diṭṭhāsavo, diṭṭhogho, cakkhāyatanaṃ, taṃ kutettha labbhā iccādi.

Here it does not apply: `pañcindriyāni`, `saddhindriyaṃ`, `sattuttamo`, `ekūnavīsati`, `yassete`, `sugatovādo`, `diṭṭhāsavo`, `diṭṭhogho`, `cakkhāyatanaṃ`, `taṃ kutettha labbhā` and so on.

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `vā` in the sense of a restricted option, the rule has an `asanta` domain, where the following vowel is never elided; in these words the preceding vowel drops by the general rule [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13), and where the survivor is `i` or `u` after `a`/`ā` it may then become `e`/`o` by [16 (§14): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r16) or lengthen by [17 (§15): Dīghaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r17).

| words | preceding vowel elided by §12 | result | never |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pañca indriyāni | ~~a~~ | **pañcindriyāni** | pañcandriyāni |
| saddhā indriyaṃ | ~~ā~~ | **saddhindriyaṃ** | saddhāndriyaṃ |
| satta uttamo | ~~a~~ | **sattuttamo** | sattattamo |
| eka ūna vīsati | ~~a~~ | **ekūnavīsati** | ekanavīsati |
| yassa ete | ~~a~~ | **yassete** | yassate |
| sugata ovādo | ~~a~~ | **sugatovādo** | sugatavādo |
| diṭṭhi āsavo | ~~i~~ | **diṭṭhāsavo** | diṭṭhisavo |
| diṭṭhi ogho | ~~i~~ | **diṭṭhogho** | diṭṭhigho |
| cakkhu āyatanaṃ | ~~u~~ | **cakkhāyatanaṃ** | cakkhuyatanaṃ |
| kuto ettha | ~~o~~ | **kutettha** | kutottha |

> `taṃ kutettha labbhā` \
> [(1V Veranjakanda: Verañjakaṇḍa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/Veranjakanda#36) \
🚻①⨀(ta) 🔼(kuto) 🔼(ettha) 🔼(labbhā) \
that | whence | here | could be had

> `yassete` \
> [(12S1 10.1.12: Āḷavakasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/10/10.1/10.1.12#1520) \
🚹⑥⨀(ya) 🚹①⨂(eta) \
whose | these

:::

> Bhavati ca vavatthitavibhāsāya.

And there is [this summary verse] on the restricted option (`vavatthitavibhāsā`):

> Avaṇṇato sarodānī-tīvevādiṃ vinā paro; \
> Na luppataññato dīgho, āsevādivivajjito.

After an `a`-vaṇṇa the following vowel is not elided, except [the initial of] `idāni`, `iti`, `iva`, `eva` and the like; \
after another [vowel] a following long vowel is not elided, leaving aside `āsi`, `eva` and the like.

> Bandhussa iva, upa ikkhati, upa ito, ava icca, jina īritaṃ, na upeti, canda udayo, yathā udake itīdha pubbāvaṇṇassarānaṃ lope kate ‘‘paro, asarūpe’’ti ca vattate, tathā ‘‘ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā’’ti ito ivaṇṇa ggahaṇañca, ‘‘vamodudantāna’’nti ito u ggahaṇañca sīhagatiyā idhānuvattetabbaṃ.

Here, in `bandhussa iva`, `upa ikkhati`, `upa ito`, `ava icca`, `jina īritaṃ`, `na upeti`, `canda udayo` and `yathā udake`, when the elision of the preceding `a`-vaṇṇa vowel has been made, `paro` and `asarūpe` continue [into the next rule]; likewise the word `ivaṇṇa` from [21 (§21): Ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r21) and the word `u` from [20 (§18): Vamodudantānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r20) are to be carried here by a lion's glance (`sīhagati`, a later rule looking back).

---

<a name="r16"></a>

### 16 (§14): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte (Occasionally, different vaṇṇa after elision) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§14 (16): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m14" description="Occasionally, different vaṇṇa after elision" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second exception, in Kaccāyana's place: once the preceding vowel has dropped by [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13), a surviving `i`, `ī` or `u` may become `e` or `o`. Buddhappiya's gloss names the mechanism — `e` and `o` are the `asavaṇṇa`, the vowels that have no homogeneous partner, and each is the vowel nearest in place of articulation to the one it replaces (`ṭhānāsannavasena`) — and adds two things the Kaccāyana vutti does not say. First, he assembles the rule's subject from two later rules by a lion's glance, as announced at the end of [15 (§13): Vā paro asarūpā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r15): `ivaṇṇa` from [21 (§21): Ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r21) and `u` (not `u`-vaṇṇa: `ū` never becomes `o`) from [20 (§18): Vamodudantānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r20). Second, he limits the rule to the case where the elided vowel was `a` or `ā`: the `vā` of 15 is still in force, but the use of `kvaci` cancels it here (`kvaci karaṇato`), so `diṭṭhi upādānaṃ` gives `diṭṭhupādānaṃ`, not `diṭṭhopādānaṃ`, and `yo me issaro` gives `yo missaro`. `kvaci` is the exception marker of *Boundaries and Domains* §4.2: it isolates from the general state of affairs the words in which the change does happen (`bandhusseva`, `upekkhati`, `candodayo`) and leaves `tatrime`, `yassindriyāni`, `lokuttaro` to the plain elision of 13. The three `ti kiṃ?` questions test `kvaci`, `lutte` and `asarūpe` in turn. The last two blocks list the twenty-two phrases of the next rule and carry `kvaci` (as an `adhikāra`), `paro` and `lutte` into it.

:::

> I vaṇṇabhūto, u kārabhūto ca paro saro asarūpe pubbassare lutte kvaci asavaṇṇaṃ pappoti. Natthi savaṇṇā etesanti asavaṇṇā, ekārokārā, tattha ṭhānāsannavasena ivaṇṇukārānamekārokārā honti.

A following vowel that is an `i`-vaṇṇa, or that is `u`, when the preceding dissimilar vowel has been elided, in some places (`kvaci`) becomes the non-homogeneous [vowel]. `asavaṇṇa` are those that have no homogeneous [partner], namely `e` and `o`; there, by nearness of place [of articulation], `i`-vaṇṇa becomes `e` and `u` becomes `o`.

> Bandhusseva, upekkhati, upeto, avecca, jineritaṃ, nopeti, candodayo, yathodake.

`bandhusseva`, `upekkhati`, `upeto`, `avecca`, `jineritaṃ`, `nopeti`, `candodayo`, `yathodake`.

:::tip[Examples]

| words | elided by §12 | changed by §14 | result | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| bandhussa iva | ~~a~~ | i→e | **bandhusseva** | like a kinsman's |
| upa ikkhati | ~~a~~ | i→e | **upekkhati** | he looks on |
| upa ito | ~~a~~ | i→e | **upeto** | endowed with |
| ava icca | ~~a~~ | i→e | **avecca** | having understood |
| jina īritaṃ | ~~a~~ | ī→e | **jineritaṃ** | spoken by the Conqueror |
| na upeti | ~~a~~ | u→o | **nopeti** | it does not approach |
| canda udayo | ~~a~~ | u→o | **candodayo** | moonrise |
| yathā udake | ~~ā~~ | u→o | **yathodake** | as in water |

|     | bandhussa + iva               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bandhuss + (a) + (i) + va     | §10  |
| →   | bandhuss + (~~a~~ + i) + va   | §12  |
| →   | bandhuss + (i→e) + va         | §14  |
| →   | (bandhuss + e) + va           | §11  |
| →   | **bandhusseva**               |      |

|     | canda + udayo                 | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | cand + (a) + (u) + dayo       | §10  |
| →   | cand + (~~a~~ + u) + dayo     | §12  |
| →   | cand + (u→o) + dayo           | §14  |
| →   | (cand + o) + dayo             | §11  |
| →   | **candodayo**                 |      |

> `yathodake āvile appasanne` \
> [(22J 2.4.5: Anabhiratijātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/2/2.4/2.4.5#450) \
🔼(yathā) 🚻⑦⨀(udaka) 🚻⑦⨀(āvila) 🚻⑦⨀(appasanna) \
as | in water | muddy | unclear

:::

> Kvacī ti kiṃ? Tatrime, yassindriyāni, mahiddhiko, sabbītiyo, tenupasaṅkami, lokuttaro. Lutte ti kiṃ? Cha ime dhammā, yathā idaṃ, kusalassa upasampadā. Asarūpe ti kiṃ? Cattārimāni, mātupaṭṭhānaṃ.

Why `kvaci`? [Because of] `tatrime`, `yassindriyāni`, `mahiddhiko`, `sabbītiyo`, `tenupasaṅkami`, `lokuttaro`. Why `lutte`? [Because of] `cha ime dhammā`, `yathā idaṃ`, `kusalassa upasampadā`. Why `asarūpe`? [Because of] `cattārimāni`, `mātupaṭṭhānaṃ`.

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `kvaci` (in some places) the change is not made everywhere the preceding `a`/`ā` has dropped: here the surviving `i` or `u` stays as it is.

| words | result | not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| tatra ime | **tatrime** | tatreme |
| yassa indriyāni | **yassindriyāni** | yassendriyāni |
| mahā iddhiko | **mahiddhiko** | maheddhiko |
| sabba ītiyo | **sabbītiyo** | sabbetiyo |
| tena upasaṅkami | **tenupasaṅkami** | tenopasaṅkami |
| loka uttaro | **lokuttaro** | lokottaro |

> `yassindriyāni samathaṅgatāni` \
> [(18Dh 7.5: Mahākaccāyanattheravatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/7/7.5#101) \
🚹⑥⨀(ya) 🚻①⨂(indriya) 🚻①⨂(samathaṅgata) \
whose | faculties | have come to calm

By saying `lutte` (when [the preceding vowel] has been elided) there is no change where no elision has taken place: `cha ime dhammā`, `yathā idaṃ`, `kusalassa upasampadā` stand unjoined.

> `kusalassa upasampadā` \
> [(7D 1.16: Cārikāanujānana)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/1/1.16#179) \
🚻⑥⨀(kusala) 🚺①⨀(upasampadā) \
of the good | the undertaking

By saying `asarūpe` (a dissimilar [vowel]) there is no change when the two vowels were homogeneous: in `cattāri imāni` and `mātu upaṭṭhānaṃ` the preceding `i` and `u` drop by [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13) and the survivors, `cattārimāni` and `mātupaṭṭhānaṃ`, keep their form.

:::

> Ettha ca satipi heṭṭhā vā ggahaṇe kvaci karaṇato avaṇṇe eva lutte idha vuttavidhi hotīti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tato idha na bhavati – diṭṭhupādānaṃ, pañcahupāli, mudindriyaṃ, yo missaroti.

And here, although the word `vā` stands in the rule before [and continues], because `kvaci` is used it is to be understood that the operation stated here takes place only when an `a`-vaṇṇa has been elided. Hence it does not apply here: `diṭṭhupādānaṃ`, `pañcahupāli`, `mudindriyaṃ`, `yo missaro`.

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `kvaci` Buddhappiya also excludes every case in which the elided vowel was not `a` or `ā`: after `i`, `e` or `u` has dropped the survivor never changes.

| words | elided by §12 | result | not |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| diṭṭhi upādānaṃ | ~~i~~ | **diṭṭhupādānaṃ** | diṭṭhopādānaṃ |
| pañcahi upāli | ~~i~~ | **pañcahupāli** | pañcahopāli |
| mudu indriyaṃ | ~~u~~ | **mudindriyaṃ** | mudendriyaṃ |
| yo me issaro | ~~e~~ | **yo missaro** | yo messaro |

> `yo missaro tattha ahosi rājā` \
> [(23J 22.1.9.9: Kāḷāgirikaṇḍa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.9/22.1.9.9#2948) \
🚹①⨀(ya) ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) 🚹①⨀(issara) 🔼(tattha) ⏮🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹①⨀(rāja) \
who | my | lord | there | was | king

> `mudindriyaṃ` \
> [(27Ne 4.2.2: Vicayahārasampāta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/27Ne/4/4.2/4.2.2#489) \
🚹②⨀(mudindriya) \
one of dull faculties

:::

> Tatra ayaṃ, buddha anussati, sa atthikā, paññavā assa, tadā ahaṃ, yāni idha bhūtāni, gacchāmi iti, ati ito, kikī iva, bahu upakāraṃ, madhu udakaṃ, su upadhāritaṃ, yopi ayaṃ, idāni ahaṃ, sace ayaṃ, appassuto ayaṃ, itara itarena, saddhā idha vittaṃ, kamma upanissayo, tathā upamaṃ, ratti uparato, vi upasamo iccatra pubbassarānaṃ lope kate –

Here, in `tatra ayaṃ`, `buddha anussati`, `sa atthikā`, `paññavā assa`, `tadā ahaṃ`, `yāni idha bhūtāni`, `gacchāmi iti`, `ati ito`, `kikī iva`, `bahu upakāraṃ`, `madhu udakaṃ`, `su upadhāritaṃ`, `yopi ayaṃ`, `idāni ahaṃ`, `sace ayaṃ`, `appassuto ayaṃ`, `itara itarena`, `saddhā idha vittaṃ`, `kamma upanissayo`, `tathā upamaṃ`, `ratti uparato` and `vi upasamo`, when the elision of the preceding vowels has been made –

> ‘‘Kvacī’’ti adhikāro, ‘‘paro, lutte’’ti ca vattate.

`kvaci` is the governing word (`adhikāra`); `paro` and `lutte` also continue.

---

<a name="r17"></a>

### 17 (§15): Dīghaṃ (Long) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§15 (17): Dīghaṃ" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m15" description="Long" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third exception, again in Kaccāyana's place: after the preceding vowel has dropped, the following one may be lengthened. Buddhappiya gives the mechanism, parallel to [16 (§14): Kvacāsavaṇṇaṃ lutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r16): by nearness of place each short vowel takes its own homogeneous long (`savaṇṇadīgha`) — `a` to `ā`, `i` to `ī`, `u` to `ū`. `kvaci`, carried from 16 as the governing word, is again the exception marker: the twenty-two examples lengthen, the thirteen `Kvacī ti kiṃ?` cases do not (`tīṇimāni`, `pañcaṅgiko`, `munindo`, `gacchāmahaṃ` …), and nothing but usage decides which is which — that is what `kvaci` says (*Boundaries and Domains* §4.1–§4.2). The `Lutte ti kiṃ?` cases show that without an elision there is no lengthening: `yathā ayaṃ`, `kikī iva` stand unjoined. Kaccāyana's vutti has three examples; Buddhappiya's list runs through the elided vowels (`a`, `ā`, `i`, `ī`, `u`, `e`, `o`) and the vowels lengthened (`a`, `i`, `u`). The last two blocks list the nine phrases of the next rule, in which it is the following vowel that has dropped, and carry `lutte` and `dīghaṃ` into it.

:::

> Saro kho paro pubbassare lutte kvaci dīghabhāvaṃ pappotīti ṭhānāsannavasena rassassarānaṃ savaṇṇadīgho.

A following vowel, when the preceding vowel has been elided, in some places becomes long — that is, by nearness of place [of articulation] the short vowels take their homogeneous long (`savaṇṇadīgha`).

> Tatrāyaṃ, buddhānussati, sātthikā, paññavāssa, tadāhaṃ, yānīdha bhūtāni, gacchāmīti, atīto, kikīva, ba hūpakāraṃ, madhūdakaṃ, sūpadhāritaṃ, yopāyaṃ, idānāhaṃ, sacāyaṃ, appassutāyaṃ, itarītarena, saddhīdha vittaṃ, kammūpanissayo, tathūpamaṃ, rattūparato, vūpasamo.

`tatrāyaṃ`, `buddhānussati`, `sātthikā`, `paññavāssa`, `tadāhaṃ`, `yānīdha bhūtāni`, `gacchāmīti`, `atīto`, `kikīva`, `bahūpakāraṃ`, `madhūdakaṃ`, `sūpadhāritaṃ`, `yopāyaṃ`, `idānāhaṃ`, `sacāyaṃ`, `appassutāyaṃ`, `itarītarena`, `saddhīdha vittaṃ`, `kammūpanissayo`, `tathūpamaṃ`, `rattūparato`, `vūpasamo`.

:::tip[Examples]

| words | elided by §12 | lengthened by §15 | result | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tatra ayaṃ | ~~a~~ | a→ā | **tatrāyaṃ** | there this |
| buddha anussati | ~~a~~ | a→ā | **buddhānussati** | recollection of the Buddha |
| sa atthikā | ~~a~~ | a→ā | **sātthikā** | with a purpose |
| paññavā assa | ~~ā~~ | a→ā | **paññavāssa** | let him be wise |
| tadā ahaṃ | ~~ā~~ | a→ā | **tadāhaṃ** | then I |
| yāni idha bhūtāni | ~~i~~ | i→ī | **yānīdha bhūtāni** | whatever beings here |
| gacchāmi iti | ~~i~~ | i→ī | **gacchāmīti** | "I go" |
| ati ito | ~~i~~ | i→ī | **atīto** | past |
| kikī iva | ~~ī~~ | i→ī | **kikīva** | like a blue jay |
| bahu upakāraṃ | ~~u~~ | u→ū | **bahūpakāraṃ** | of great help |
| madhu udakaṃ | ~~u~~ | u→ū | **madhūdakaṃ** | honey-water |
| su upadhāritaṃ | ~~u~~ | u→ū | **sūpadhāritaṃ** | well considered |
| yopi ayaṃ | ~~i~~ | a→ā | **yopāyaṃ** | and whoever this |
| idāni ahaṃ | ~~i~~ | a→ā | **idānāhaṃ** | now I |
| sace ayaṃ | ~~e~~ | a→ā | **sacāyaṃ** | if this |
| appassuto ayaṃ | ~~o~~ | a→ā | **appassutāyaṃ** | this one of little learning |
| itara itarena | ~~a~~ | i→ī | **itarītarena** | with one or the other |
| saddhā idha vittaṃ | ~~ā~~ | i→ī | **saddhīdha vittaṃ** | faith is wealth here |
| kamma upanissayo | ~~a~~ | u→ū | **kammūpanissayo** | the support of kamma |
| tathā upamaṃ | ~~ā~~ | u→ū | **tathūpamaṃ** | like that |
| ratti uparato | ~~i~~ | u→ū | **rattūparato** | abstaining at night |
| vi upasamo | ~~i~~ | u→ū | **vūpasamo** | calming |

|     | tatra + ayaṃ               | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tatr + (a) + (a) + yaṃ     | §10  |
| →   | tatr + (~~a~~ + a) + yaṃ   | §12  |
| →   | tatr + (a→ā) + yaṃ         | §15  |
| →   | (tatr + ā) + yaṃ           | §11  |
| →   | **tatrāyaṃ**               |      |

|     | saddhā + idha                | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | saddh + (ā) + (i) + dha      | §10  |
| →   | saddh + (~~ā~~ + i) + dha    | §12  |
| →   | saddh + (i→ī) + dha          | §15  |
| →   | (saddh + ī) + dha            | §11  |
| →   | **saddhīdha**                |      |

> `yānīdha bhūtāni samāgatāni` \
> [(18Kh 6: Ratanasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Kh/6#45) \
🚻①⨂(ya) 🔼(idha) 🚻①⨂(bhūta) 🚻①⨂(samāgata) \
whatever | here | beings | assembled

> `saddhīdha vittaṃ purisassa seṭṭhaṃ` \
> [(12S1 1.8.3: Vittasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/1/1.8/1.8.3#340) \
🚺①⨀(saddhā) 🔼(idha) 🚻①⨀(vitta) 🚹⑥⨀(purisa) 🚻①⨀(seṭṭha) \
faith | here | wealth | of a man | the best

> `kikīva aṇḍaṃ rakkheyya` \
> [(20Ap1 2.6: Rāhulattheraapadāna)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/20Ap1/2/2.6#788) \
🚺①⨀(kikī) 🔼(iva) 🚻②⨀(aṇḍa) ⏯🤟⨀(rakkhati) \
a blue jay | like | its egg | would guard

> `paññavāssa yathā mūgo` \
> [(19Th1 8.1.1: Mahākaccāyanattheragāthā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/19Th1/8/8.1/8.1.1#794) \
🚹①⨀(paññavantu) ⏯🤟⨀(atthi) 🔼(yathā) 🚹①⨀(mūga) \
wise | let him be | as if | dumb

:::

> Kvacī ti kiṃ? Aciraṃ vata’yaṃ kāyo, kimpimāya, tīṇimāni, pañcasupādānakkhandhesu, tassattho, pañcaṅgiko, munindo, satindriyaṃ, lahuṭṭhānaṃ, gacchāmahaṃ, tatridaṃ, pañcahupāli, natthaññaṃ. Lutte ti kiṃ? Yathā ayaṃ, nimi iva rājā, kikī iva, su upadhāritaṃ.

Why `kvaci`? [Because of] `aciraṃ vatayaṃ kāyo`, `kimpimāya`, `tīṇimāni`, `pañcasupādānakkhandhesu`, `tassattho`, `pañcaṅgiko`, `munindo`, `satindriyaṃ`, `lahuṭṭhānaṃ`, `gacchāmahaṃ`, `tatridaṃ`, `pañcahupāli`, `natthaññaṃ`. Why `lutte`? [Because of] `yathā ayaṃ`, `nimi iva rājā`, `kikī iva`, `su upadhāritaṃ`.

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `kvaci` (in some places) the lengthening is not general: in these words the preceding vowel has dropped and the survivor keeps its short measure.

| words | result | not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| aciraṃ vata ayaṃ kāyo | **aciraṃ vatayaṃ kāyo** | vatāyaṃ |
| kiṃ pi imāya | **kimpimāya** | kimpīmāya |
| tīṇi imāni | **tīṇimāni** | tīṇīmāni |
| pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu | **pañcasupādānakkhandhesu** | pañcasūpādāna° |
| tassa attho | **tassattho** | tassāttho |
| pañca aṅgiko | **pañcaṅgiko** | pañcāṅgiko |
| muni indo | **munindo** | munīndo |
| sati indriyaṃ | **satindriyaṃ** | satīndriyaṃ |
| lahu uṭṭhānaṃ | **lahuṭṭhānaṃ** | lahūṭṭhānaṃ |
| gacchāmi ahaṃ | **gacchāmahaṃ** | gacchāmāhaṃ |
| tatra idaṃ | **tatridaṃ** | tatrīdaṃ |
| pañcahi upāli | **pañcahupāli** | pañcahūpāli |
| natthi aññaṃ | **natthaññaṃ** | natthāññaṃ |

> `aciraṃ vatayaṃ kāyo` \
> [(18Dh 3.7: Pūtigattatissattheravatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/3/3.7#44) \
🔼(aciraṃ) 🔼(vata) 🚹①⨀(ima) 🚹①⨀(kāya) \
before long | alas | this | body

> `kimpimāya aññaṃ kiñci adeyyaṃ bhavissati` \
> [(3V 6.9: Manussamaṃsapaṭikkhepakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/6/6.9#1268) \
🔼(kiṃ) 🔼(pi) 🚺③⨀(ima) 🚻①⨀(añña) 🚻①⨀(kiñci) 🚻①⨀(adeyya) ⏯🤟⨀(bhavati) \
what | even | for this [woman] | other | anything | not to be given | will there be

By saying `lutte` (when [the preceding vowel] has been elided) there is no lengthening where nothing has been elided: `yathā ayaṃ`, `nimi iva rājā`, `kikī iva` and `su upadhāritaṃ` stand as they are.

:::

> Lokassa iti, deva iti, vi ati patanti, vi ati nāmenti, saṅghāṭi api, jīvitahetu api, vijju iva, kiṃsu idha vittaṃ, sādhu iti itīdha parassarānaṃ lope kate –

Here, in `lokassa iti`, `deva iti`, `vi ati patanti`, `vi ati nāmenti`, `saṅghāṭi api`, `jīvitahetu api`, `vijju iva`, `kiṃsu idha vittaṃ` and `sādhu iti`, when the elision of the following vowels has been made –

> ‘‘Lutte, dīgha’’nti ca vattate.

`lutte` and `dīghaṃ` also continue.

---

<a name="r18"></a>

### 18 (§16): Pubbo ca (And previous) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§16 (18): Pubbo ca" href="/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m16" description="And previous" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The mirror image of [17 (§15): Dīghaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r17), in Kaccāyana's place: when the following vowel has dropped — by [15 (§13): Vā paro asarūpā](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r15), which is why every second word in the nine phrases begins with `i`, `a` or `e` of `iti`, `api`, `idha`, `iva` or `ati` — the preceding vowel may lengthen. Buddhappiya reads the sutta's `ca` not as an extension but as `anukaḍḍhana`, "dragging in": it pulls `lutte` and `dīghaṃ` down from the rule before, and its purpose is to signal the maxim that what `ca` drags in goes no further (`cānukaḍḍhitaṃ uttaratra nānuvattate`), so that `lutte` and `dīghaṃ` stop here and are not available to [19 (§17): Yamedantassādeso](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r19), whose lengthening he will take from the consonant-sandhi rule [37 (§25): Dīghaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r37) instead. `kvaci`, still the governing word, is again the exception marker: `lokassāti`, `vītipatanti`, `kiṃsūdha vittaṃ` lengthen, but `yassadāni`, `itissa`, `idānipi`, `tesupi`, `cakkhundriyaṃ`, `kinnumāva` do not, though the following vowel has dropped in each. The closing block lists the five phrases of the next rule, in which the elision of the preceding vowel would be due.

:::

> Pubbo saro parassare lutte kvacidīghaṃ pappoti. Ca ggahaṇaṃ luttadīghaggahaṇānukaḍḍhanatthaṃ, taṃ ‘‘cā nukaḍḍhitamuttaratra nānuvattate’’ti ñāpanatthaṃ.

The preceding vowel, when the following vowel has been elided, in some places becomes long. The word `ca` is for dragging in (`anukaḍḍhana`) the words `lutte` and `dīghaṃ` [from the rules before], and that in order to make known [the maxim] "what is dragged in by `ca` does not continue further on".

> Lokassāti, devāti, vītipatanti, vītināmenti, saṅghāṭīpi, jīvitahetūpi, vijjūva, kiṃsūdha vittaṃ, sādhūti.

`lokassāti`, `devāti`, `vītipatanti`, `vītināmenti`, `saṅghāṭīpi`, `jīvitahetūpi`, `vijjūva`, `kiṃsūdha vittaṃ`, `sādhūti`.

:::tip[Examples]

| words | following vowel elided by §13 | lengthened by §16 | result | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| lokassa iti | ~~i~~ | a→ā | **lokassāti** | "of the world" |
| deva iti | ~~i~~ | a→ā | **devāti** | "O king" |
| vi ati patanti | ~~a~~ | i→ī | **vītipatanti** | they flit about |
| vi ati nāmenti | ~~a~~ | i→ī | **vītināmenti** | they pass [the time] |
| saṅghāṭi api | ~~a~~ | i→ī | **saṅghāṭīpi** | the outer robe too |
| jīvitahetu api | ~~a~~ | u→ū | **jīvitahetūpi** | even for life's sake |
| vijju iva | ~~i~~ | u→ū | **vijjūva** | like lightning |
| kiṃsu idha vittaṃ | ~~i~~ | u→ū | **kiṃsūdha vittaṃ** | what here is wealth |
| sādhu iti | ~~i~~ | u→ū | **sādhūti** | "good" |

|     | lokassa + iti               | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | lokass + (a) + (i) + ti     | §10  |
| →   | lokass + (a + ~~i~~) + ti   | §13  |
| →   | lokass + (a→ā) + ti         | §16  |
| →   | (lokass + ā) + ti           | §11  |
| →   | **lokassāti**               |      |

|     | kiṃsu + idha                | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kiṃs + (u) + (i) + dha      | §10  |
| →   | kiṃs + (u + ~~i~~) + dha    | §13  |
| →   | kiṃs + (u→ū) + dha          | §16  |
| →   | (kiṃs + ū) + dha            | §11  |
| →   | **kiṃsūdha**                |      |

> `vītipatanti` \
> [(18Sn 3.11: Nālakasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/3/3.11#826) \
▶️🤟⨂(vītipatati) \
they flit about

> `kiṃsūdha vittaṃ purisassa seṭṭhaṃ` \
> [(12S1 1.8.3: Vittasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/1/1.8/1.8.3#339) \
🔼(kiṃsu) 🔼(idha) 🚻①⨀(vitta) 🚹⑥⨀(purisa) 🚻①⨀(seṭṭha) \
what | here | wealth | of a man | the best

> `jīvitahetūpi` \
> [(21Cp 2.1.5: Mahiṃsarājacariya)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/21Cp/2/2.1/2.1.5#210) \
🔼(jīvitahetu) 🔼(pi) \
for life's sake | even

> `vijjūva` \
> [(21Bu 17: Dhammadassībuddhavaṃsa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/21Bu/17#743) \
🚺①⨀(vijju) 🔼(iva) \
lightning | like

:::

> Kvacī ti kiṃ? Yassadāni, itissa, idānipi, tesupi, cakkhundriyaṃ, kinnumāva.

Why `kvaci`? [Because of] `yassadāni`, `itissa`, `idānipi`, `tesupi`, `cakkhundriyaṃ`, `kinnumāva`.

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `kvaci` (in some places) the preceding vowel does not lengthen everywhere the following one has dropped:

| words | result | not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| yassa idāni | **yassadāni** | yassādāni |
| iti assa | **itissa** | itīssa |
| idāni api | **idānipi** | idānīpi |
| tesu api | **tesupi** | tesūpi |
| cakkhu indriyaṃ | **cakkhundriyaṃ** | cakkhūndriyaṃ |
| kiṃ nu imā eva | **kinnumāva** | kinnūmāva |

> `itissa muhuttampi` \
> [(2V 1.5.8.7: Sañciccasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.8/1.5.8.7#1381) \
🔼(iti) 🚹⑥⨀(ima) 🚹②⨀(muhutta) 🔼(pi) \
thus | for him | a moment | even

> `kinnumāva samaṇiyo` \
